===== M0774 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ClosureCodingConvention.getObjectLiteralCast(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ClosureCodingConvention.java
Original range: 326-352
Documentation:

Method code:
326 |   @Override
327 |   public ObjectLiteralCast getObjectLiteralCast(Node callNode) {
328 |     Preconditions.checkArgument(callNode.isCall());
329 |     ObjectLiteralCast proxyCast = super.getObjectLiteralCast(callNode);
330 |     if (proxyCast != null) {
331 |       return proxyCast;
332 |     }
333 | 
334 |     Node callName = callNode.getFirstChild();
335 |     if (!"goog.reflect.object".equals(callName.getQualifiedName()) ||
336 |         callNode.getChildCount() != 3) {
337 |       return null;
338 |     }
339 | 
340 |     Node typeNode = callName.getNext();
341 |     if (!typeNode.isQualifiedName()) {
342 |       return null;
343 |     }
344 | 
345 |     Node objectNode = typeNode.getNext();
346 |     if (!objectNode.isObjectLit()) {
347 |       return new ObjectLiteralCast(null, null, OBJECTLIT_EXPECTED);
348 |     }
349 | 
350 |     return new ObjectLiteralCast(

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.JsDocInfoParser.parseModifiesTag(JsDocToken)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/JsDocInfoParser.java
Original range: 1148-1187
Documentation:
/**
   * Parse a {@code @modifies} tag of the form
   * {@code @modifies&#123;this|arguments|param&#125;}.
   *
   * @param token The current token.
   */
Method code:
1148 |   private JsDocToken parseModifiesTag(JsDocToken token) {
1149 |     if (token == JsDocToken.LC) {
1150 |       Set<String> modifies = new HashSet<String>();
1151 |       while (true) {
1152 |         if (match(JsDocToken.STRING)) {
1153 |           String name = stream.getString();
1154 |           if (!modifiesAnnotationKeywords.contains(name)
1155 |               && !jsdocBuilder.hasParameter(name)) {
1156 |               parser.addParserWarning("msg.jsdoc.modifies.unknown", name,
1157 |                   stream.getLineno(), stream.getCharno());
1158 |           }
1159 | 
1160 |           modifies.add(stream.getString());
1161 |           token = next();
1162 |         } else {
1163 |           parser.addParserWarning("msg.jsdoc.modifies",
1164 |               stream.getLineno(), stream.getCharno());
1165 |           return token;
1166 |         }
1167 | 
1168 |         if (match(JsDocToken.PIPE)) {
1169 |           token = next();
1170 |         } else {
1171 |           break;
1172 |         }
1173 |       }
1174 | 
1175 |       if (!match(JsDocToken.RC)) {
1176 |         parser.addParserWarning("msg.jsdoc.modifies",
1177 |             stream.getLineno(), stream.getCharno());
1178 |       } else {
1179 |         token = next();
1180 |         if (!jsdocBuilder.recordModifies(modifies)) {
1181 |           parser.addParserWarning("msg.jsdoc.modifies.duplicate",
1182 |               stream.getLineno(), stream.getCharno());
1183 |         }
1184 |       }
1185 |     }
1186 |     return token;
1187 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.FunctionTypeBuilder.inferInheritance(JSDocInfo)

Original range: 335-426
Documentation:
/**
   * Infer the role of the function (whether it's a constructor or interface)
   * and what it inherits from in JSDocInfo.
   */
Method code:
335 |   FunctionTypeBuilder inferInheritance(@Nullable JSDocInfo info) {
336 |     if (info != null) {
337 |       isConstructor = info.isConstructor();
338 |       makesStructs = info.makesStructs();
339 |       makesDicts = info.makesDicts();
340 |       isInterface = info.isInterface();
341 | 
342 |       if (makesStructs && !isConstructor) {
343 |         reportWarning(CONSTRUCTOR_REQUIRED, "@struct", formatFnName());
344 |       } else if (makesDicts && !isConstructor) {
345 |         reportWarning(CONSTRUCTOR_REQUIRED, "@dict", formatFnName());
346 |       }
347 | 
348 |       // Class template types, which can be used in the scope of a constructor
349 |       // definition.
350 |       ImmutableList<String> typeParameters = info.getTemplateTypeNames();
351 |       if (!typeParameters.isEmpty()) {
352 |         if (isConstructor || isInterface) {
353 |           ImmutableList.Builder<TemplateType> builder = ImmutableList.builder();
354 |           for (String typeParameter : typeParameters) {
355 |             builder.add(typeRegistry.createTemplateType(typeParameter));
356 |           }
357 |           classTemplateTypeNames = builder.build();
358 |           typeRegistry.setTemplateTypeNames(classTemplateTypeNames);
359 |         }
360 |       }
361 | 
362 |       // base type
363 |       if (info.hasBaseType()) {
364 |         if (isConstructor) {
365 |           JSType maybeBaseType =
366 |               info.getBaseType().evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
367 |           if (maybeBaseType != null &&
368 |               maybeBaseType.setValidator(new ExtendedTypeValidator())) {
369 |             baseType = (ObjectType) maybeBaseType;
370 |           }
371 |         } else {
372 |           reportWarning(EXTENDS_WITHOUT_TYPEDEF, formatFnName());
373 |         }
374 |       }
375 | 
376 |       // Implemented interfaces (for constructors only).
377 |       if (info.getImplementedInterfaceCount() > 0) {
378 |         if (isConstructor) {
379 |           implementedInterfaces = Lists.newArrayList();
380 |           Set<JSType> baseInterfaces = new HashSet<JSType>();
381 |           for (JSTypeExpression t : info.getImplementedInterfaces()) {
382 |             JSType maybeInterType = t.evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
383 | 
384 |             if (maybeInterType != null &&
385 |                 maybeInterType.setValidator(new ImplementedTypeValidator())) {
386 |               // Disallow implementing the same base (not templatized) interface
387 |               // type more than once.
388 |               JSType baseInterface = maybeInterType;
389 |               if (baseInterface.toMaybeTemplatizedType() != null) {
390 |                 baseInterface =
391 |                     baseInterface.toMaybeTemplatizedType().getReferencedType();
392 |               }
393 |               if (baseInterfaces.contains(baseInterface)) {
394 |                 reportWarning(SAME_INTERFACE_MULTIPLE_IMPLEMENTS,
395 |                               baseInterface.toString());
396 |               } else {
397 |                 baseInterfaces.add(baseInterface);
398 |               }
399 | 
400 |               implementedInterfaces.add((ObjectType) maybeInterType);
401 |             }
402 |           }
403 |         } else if (isInterface) {
404 |           reportWarning(
405 |               TypeCheck.CONFLICTING_IMPLEMENTED_TYPE, formatFnName());
406 |         } else {
407 |           reportWarning(CONSTRUCTOR_REQUIRED, "@implements", formatFnName());
408 |         }
409 |       }
410 | 
411 |       // extended interfaces (for interfaces only)
412 |       // We've already emitted a warning if this is not an interface.
413 |       if (isInterface) {
414 |         extendedInterfaces = Lists.newArrayList();
415 |         for (JSTypeExpression t : info.getExtendedInterfaces()) {
416 |           JSType maybeInterfaceType = t.evaluate(scope, typeRegistry);
417 |           if (maybeInterfaceType != null &&
418 |               maybeInterfaceType.setValidator(new ExtendedTypeValidator())) {
419 |             extendedInterfaces.add((ObjectType) maybeInterfaceType);
420 |           }
421 |         }
422 |       }
423 |     }
424 | 
425 |     return this;
426 |   }

===== M0792 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.JsDocInfoParser.parseTypeExpression(JsDocToken)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/JsDocInfoParser.java
Original range: 1842-1887
Documentation:
/**
   * TypeExpression := BasicTypeExpression
   *     | '?' BasicTypeExpression
   *     | '!' BasicTypeExpression
   *     | BasicTypeExpression '?'
   *     | BasicTypeExpression '!'
   *     | '?'
   */
Method code:
1842 |   private Node parseTypeExpression(JsDocToken token) {
1843 |     if (token == JsDocToken.QMARK) {
1844 |       // A QMARK could mean that a type is nullable, or that it's unknown.
1845 |       // We use look-ahead 1 to determine whether it's unknown. Otherwise,
1846 |       // we assume it means nullable. There are 8 cases:
1847 |       // {?} - right curly
1848 |       // {?=} - equals
1849 |       // {function(?, number)} - comma
1850 |       // {function(number, ?)} - right paren
1851 |       // {function(number, ...[?])} - right bracket
1852 |       // {function(): ?|number} - pipe
1853 |       // {Array.<?>} - greater than
1854 |       // /** ? */ - EOC (inline types)
1855 |       // I'm not a big fan of using look-ahead for this, but it makes
1856 |       // the type language a lot nicer.
1857 |       token = next();
1858 |       if (token == JsDocToken.COMMA ||
1859 |           token == JsDocToken.EQUALS ||
1860 |           token == JsDocToken.RB ||
1861 |           token == JsDocToken.RC ||
1862 |           token == JsDocToken.RP ||
1863 |           token == JsDocToken.PIPE ||
1864 |           token == JsDocToken.GT ||
1865 |           token == JsDocToken.EOC) {
1866 |         restoreLookAhead(token);
1867 |         return newNode(Token.QMARK);
1868 |       }
1869 | 
1870 |       return wrapNode(Token.QMARK, parseBasicTypeExpression(token));
1871 |     } else if (token == JsDocToken.BANG) {
1872 |       return wrapNode(Token.BANG, parseBasicTypeExpression(next()));
1873 |     } else {
1874 |       Node basicTypeExpr = parseBasicTypeExpression(token);
1875 |       if (basicTypeExpr != null) {
1876 |         if (match(JsDocToken.QMARK)) {
1877 |           next();
1878 |           return wrapNode(Token.QMARK, basicTypeExpr);
1879 |         } else if (match(JsDocToken.BANG)) {
1880 |           next();
1881 |           return wrapNode(Token.BANG, basicTypeExpr);
1882 |         }
1883 |       }
1884 | 
1885 |       return basicTypeExpr;
1886 |     }
1887 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.prioritizeFromEntryNode(DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 196-215
Documentation:
/**
   * Given an entry node, find all the nodes reachable from that node
   * and prioritize them.
   */
Method code:
196 |   private void prioritizeFromEntryNode(DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry) {
197 |     PriorityQueue<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>> worklist =
198 |         new PriorityQueue<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>>(10, priorityComparator);
199 |     worklist.add(entry);
200 | 
201 |     while (!worklist.isEmpty()) {
202 |       DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> current = worklist.remove();
203 |       if (nodePriorities.containsKey(current)) {
204 |         continue;
205 |       }
206 | 
207 |       nodePriorities.put(current, ++priorityCounter);
208 | 
209 |       List<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>> successors =
210 |           cfg.getDirectedSuccNodes(current);
211 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : successors) {
212 |         worklist.add(candidate);
213 |       }
214 |     }
215 |   }

/**
   * Gets the least supertype of {@code this} and {@code that}.
   * The least supertype is the join (&#8744;) or supremum of both types in the
   * type lattice.<p>
   * Examples:
   * <ul>
   * <li>{@code number &#8744; *} = {@code *}</li>
   * <li>{@code number &#8744; Object} = {@code (number, Object)}</li>
   * <li>{@code Number &#8744; Object} = {@code Object}</li>
   * </ul>
   * @return {@code this &#8744; that}
   */
Method code:
915 |   public JSType getLeastSupertype(JSType that) {
916 |     if (that.isUnionType()) {
917 |       // Union types have their own implementation of getLeastSupertype.
918 |       return that.toMaybeUnionType().getLeastSupertype(this);
919 |     }
920 |     return getLeastSupertype(this, that);
921 |   }

Documentation:
/**
   * Tells the type system that {@code owner} may have a property named
   * {@code propertyName}. This allows the registry to keep track of what
   * types a property is defined upon.
   *
   * This is NOT the same as saying that {@code owner} must have a property
   * named type. ObjectType#hasProperty attempts to minimize false positives
   * ("if we're not sure, then don't type check this property"). The type
   * registry, on the other hand, should attempt to minimize false negatives
   * ("if this property is assigned anywhere in the program, it must
   * show up in the type registry").
   */
Method code:
630 |   public void registerPropertyOnType(String propertyName, JSType type) {
631 |     UnionTypeBuilder typeSet = typesIndexedByProperty.get(propertyName);
632 |     if (typeSet == null) {
633 |       typeSet = new UnionTypeBuilder(this, PROPERTY_CHECKING_UNION_SIZE);
634 |       typesIndexedByProperty.put(propertyName, typeSet);
635 |     }
636 | 
637 |     typeSet.addAlternate(type);
638 |     addReferenceTypeIndexedByProperty(propertyName, type);
639 | 
640 |     // Clear cached values that depend on typesIndexedByProperty.
641 |     greatestSubtypeByProperty.remove(propertyName);
642 |   }

===== M0836 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.hasUnknownOrEmptySupertype(FunctionType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 1295-1317
Documentation:
/**
   * Given a constructor or an interface type, find out whether the unknown
   * type is a supertype of the current type.
   */
Method code:
1295 |   private static boolean hasUnknownOrEmptySupertype(FunctionType ctor) {
1296 |     Preconditions.checkArgument(ctor.isConstructor() || ctor.isInterface());
1297 |     Preconditions.checkArgument(!ctor.isUnknownType());
1298 | 
1299 |     // The type system should notice inheritance cycles on its own
1300 |     // and break the cycle.
1301 |     while (true) {
1302 |       ObjectType maybeSuperInstanceType =
1303 |           ctor.getPrototype().getImplicitPrototype();
1304 |       if (maybeSuperInstanceType == null) {
1305 |         return false;
1306 |       }
1307 |       if (maybeSuperInstanceType.isUnknownType() ||
1308 |           maybeSuperInstanceType.isEmptyType()) {
1309 |         return true;
1310 |       }
1311 |       ctor = maybeSuperInstanceType.getConstructor();
1312 |       if (ctor == null) {
1313 |         return false;
1314 |       }
1315 |       Preconditions.checkState(ctor.isConstructor() || ctor.isInterface());
1316 |     }
1317 |   }

199 |   @Override
200 |   public JSType caseUnionType(UnionType type) {
201 |     boolean changed = false;
202 |     List<JSType> results = Lists.newArrayList();
203 |     for (JSType alternative : type.getAlternates()) {
204 |       JSType replacement = alternative.visit(this);
205 |       if (replacement != alternative) {
206 |         changed = true;
207 |       }
208 |       results.add(replacement);
209 |     }
210 | 
211 |     if (changed) {
212 |       UnionTypeBuilder builder = new UnionTypeBuilder(registry);
213 |       for (JSType alternate : results) {
214 |         builder.addAlternate(alternate);
215 |       }
216 |       return builder.build();  // maybe not a union
217 |     }
218 | 
219 |     return type;
220 |   }

Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSTypeRegistry.java
Original range: 927-952
Documentation:
/**
   * Looks up a type by name. To allow for forward references to types, an
   * unrecognized string has to be bound to a NamedType object that will be
   * resolved later.
   *
   * @param scope A scope for doing type name resolution.
   * @param jsTypeName The name string.
   * @param sourceName The name of the source file where this reference appears.
   * @param lineno The line number of the reference.
   * @return a NamedType if the string argument is not one of the known types,
   *     otherwise the corresponding JSType object.
   */
Method code:
927 |   public JSType getType(StaticScope<JSType> scope, String jsTypeName,
928 |       String sourceName, int lineno, int charno) {
929 |     // Resolve template type names
930 |     JSType type = null;
931 |     JSType thisType = null;
932 |     if (scope != null && scope.getTypeOfThis() != null) {
933 |       thisType = scope.getTypeOfThis().toObjectType();
934 |     }
935 |     if (thisType != null) {
936 |       type = thisType.getTemplateTypeMap().getTemplateTypeKeyByName(jsTypeName);
937 |       if (type != null) {
938 |         Preconditions.checkState(type.isTemplateType(), "expected:" + type);
939 |         return type;
940 |       }
941 |     }
942 | 
943 |     type = getType(jsTypeName);
944 |     if (type == null) {
945 |       // TODO(user): Each instance should support named type creation using
946 |       // interning.
947 |       NamedType namedType = createNamedType(jsTypeName, sourceName, lineno, charno);
948 |       unresolvedNamedTypes.put(scope, namedType);
949 |       type = namedType;
950 |     }
951 |     return type;
952 |   }

===== M0858 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.process(Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 148-190
Documentation:

Method code:
148 |   @Override
149 |   public void process(Node externs, Node root) {
150 |     this.root = root;
151 |     astPositionCounter = 0;
152 |     astPosition = Maps.newHashMap();
153 |     nodePriorities = Maps.newHashMap();
154 |     cfg = new AstControlFlowGraph(computeFallThrough(root), nodePriorities,
155 |                                   edgeAnnotations);
156 |     NodeTraversal.traverse(compiler, root, this);
157 |     astPosition.put(null, ++astPositionCounter); // the implicit return is last.
158 | 
159 |     // Now, generate the priority of nodes by doing a depth-first
160 |     // search on the CFG.
161 |     priorityCounter = 0;
162 |     DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> entry = cfg.getEntry();
163 |     prioritizeFromEntryNode(entry);
164 | 
165 |     if (shouldTraverseFunctions) {
166 |       // If we're traversing inner functions, we need to rank the
167 |       // priority of them too.
168 |       for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
169 |         Node value = candidate.getValue();
170 |         if (value != null && value.isFunction()) {
171 |           Preconditions.checkState(
172 |               !nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate) || candidate == entry);
173 |           prioritizeFromEntryNode(candidate);
174 |         }
175 |       }
176 |     }
177 | 
178 |     // At this point, all reachable nodes have been given a priority, but
179 |     // unreachable nodes have not been given a priority. Put them last.
180 |     // Presumably, it doesn't really matter what priority they get, since
181 |     // this shouldn't happen in real code.
182 |     for (DiGraphNode<Node, Branch> candidate : cfg.getDirectedGraphNodes()) {
183 |       if (!nodePriorities.containsKey(candidate)) {
184 |         nodePriorities.put(candidate, ++priorityCounter);
185 |       }
186 |     }
187 | 
188 |     // Again, the implicit return node is always last.
189 |     nodePriorities.put(cfg.getImplicitReturn(), ++priorityCounter);
190 |   }

===== M0875 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.visitGetProp(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 1404-1420
Documentation:
/**
   * Visits a GETPROP node.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal object that supplies context, such as the
   * scope chain to use in name lookups as well as error reporting.
   * @param n The node being visited.
   * @param parent The parent of <code>n</code>
   */
Method code:
1404 |   private void visitGetProp(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
1405 |     // obj.prop or obj.method()
1406 |     // Lots of types can appear on the left, a call to a void function can
1407 |     // never be on the left. getPropertyType will decide what is acceptable
1408 |     // and what isn't.
1409 |     Node property = n.getLastChild();
1410 |     Node objNode = n.getFirstChild();
1411 |     JSType childType = getJSType(objNode);
1412 | 
1413 |     if (childType.isDict()) {
1414 |       report(t, property, TypeValidator.ILLEGAL_PROPERTY_ACCESS, "'.'", "dict");

1416 |         "No properties on this expression", getNativeType(OBJECT_TYPE))) {
1417 |       checkPropertyAccess(childType, property.getString(), t, n);
1418 |     }
1419 |     ensureTyped(t, n);
1420 |   }

===== M0876 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 217-292
Documentation:

Method code:
217 |   @Override
218 |   public boolean shouldTraverse(
219 |       NodeTraversal nodeTraversal, Node n, Node parent) {
220 |     astPosition.put(n, astPositionCounter++);
221 | 
222 |     switch (n.getType()) {
223 |       case Token.FUNCTION:
224 |         if (shouldTraverseFunctions || n == cfg.getEntry().getValue()) {
225 |           exceptionHandler.push(n);
226 |           return true;
227 |         }
228 |         return false;
229 |       case Token.TRY:
230 |         exceptionHandler.push(n);
231 |         return true;
232 |     }
233 | 
234 |     /*
235 |      * We are going to stop the traversal depending on what the node's parent
236 |      * is.
237 |      *
238 |      * We are only interested in adding edges between nodes that change control
239 |      * flow. The most obvious ones are loops and IF-ELSE's. A statement
240 |      * transfers control to its next sibling.
241 |      *
242 |      * In case of an expression tree, there is no control flow within the tree
243 |      * even when there are short circuited operators and conditionals. When we
244 |      * are doing data flow analysis, we will simply synthesize lattices up the
245 |      * expression tree by finding the meet at each expression node.
246 |      *
247 |      * For example: within a Token.SWITCH, the expression in question does not
248 |      * change the control flow and need not to be considered.
249 |      */
250 |     if (parent != null) {
251 |       switch (parent.getType()) {
252 |         case Token.FOR:
253 |           // Only traverse the body of the for loop.
254 |           return n == parent.getLastChild();
255 | 
256 |         // Skip the conditions.
257 |         case Token.IF:
258 |         case Token.WHILE:
259 |         case Token.WITH:
260 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
261 |         case Token.DO:
262 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild().getNext();
263 |         // Only traverse the body of the cases
264 |         case Token.SWITCH:
265 |         case Token.CASE:
266 |         case Token.CATCH:
267 |         case Token.LABEL:
268 |           return n != parent.getFirstChild();
269 |         case Token.FUNCTION:
270 |           return n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
271 |         case Token.CONTINUE:
272 |         case Token.BREAK:
273 |         case Token.EXPR_RESULT:
274 |         case Token.VAR:
275 |         case Token.RETURN:
276 |         case Token.THROW:
277 |           return false;
278 |         case Token.TRY:
279 |           /* Just before we are about to visit the second child of the TRY node,
280 |            * we know that we will be visiting either the CATCH or the FINALLY.
281 |            * In other words, we know that the post order traversal of the TRY
282 |            * block has been finished, no more exceptions can be caught by the
283 |            * handler at this TRY block and should be taken out of the stack.
284 |            */
285 |           if (n == parent.getFirstChild().getNext()) {
286 |             Preconditions.checkState(exceptionHandler.peek() == parent);
287 |             exceptionHandler.pop();
288 |           }
289 |       }
290 |     }
291 |     return true;
292 |   }

===== M0880 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeInference.traverseName(Node,FlowScope)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeInference.java
Original range: 679-732
Documentation:

Method code:
679 |   private FlowScope traverseName(Node n, FlowScope scope) {
680 |     String varName = n.getString();
681 |     Node value = n.getFirstChild();
682 |     JSType type = n.getJSType();
683 |     if (value != null) {
684 |       scope = traverse(value, scope);
685 |       updateScopeForTypeChange(scope, n, n.getJSType() /* could be null */,
686 |           getJSType(value));
687 |       return scope;
688 |     } else {
689 |       StaticSlot<JSType> var = scope.getSlot(varName);
690 |       if (var != null) {
691 |         // There are two situations where we don't want to use type information
692 |         // from the scope, even if we have it.
693 | 
694 |         // 1) The var is escaped and assigned in an inner scope, e.g.,
695 |         // function f() { var x = 3; function g() { x = null } (x); }
696 |         boolean isInferred = var.isTypeInferred();
697 |         boolean unflowable = isInferred &&
698 |             isUnflowable(syntacticScope.getVar(varName));
699 | 
700 |         // 2) We're reading type information from another scope for an
701 |         // inferred variable. That variable is assigned more than once,
702 |         // and we can't know which type we're getting.
703 |         //
704 |         // var t = null; function f() { (t); } doStuff(); t = {};
705 |         //
706 |         // Notice that this heuristic isn't perfect. For example, you might
707 |         // have:
708 |         //
709 |         // function f() { (t); } f(); var t = 3;
710 |         //
711 |         // In this case, we would infer the first reference to t as
712 |         // type {number}, even though it's undefined.
713 |         boolean nonLocalInferredSlot = false;
714 |         if (isInferred && syntacticScope.isLocal()) {
715 |           Var maybeOuterVar = syntacticScope.getParent().getVar(varName);
716 |           if (var == maybeOuterVar &&
717 |               !maybeOuterVar.isMarkedAssignedExactlyOnce()) {
718 |             nonLocalInferredSlot = true;
719 |           }
720 |         }
721 | 
722 |         if (!unflowable && !nonLocalInferredSlot) {
723 |           type = var.getType();
724 |           if (type == null) {
725 |             type = unknownType;
726 |           }
727 |         }
728 |       }
729 |     }
730 |     n.setJSType(type);
731 |     return scope;
732 |   }

===== M0884 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.InferJSDocInfo.visit(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/InferJSDocInfo.java
Original range: 92-189
Documentation:

Method code:
92 |   @Override
93 |   public void visit(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
94 |     JSDocInfo docInfo;
95 | 
96 |     switch (n.getType()) {
97 |       // Infer JSDocInfo on types of all type declarations on variables.
98 |       case Token.NAME:
99 |         if (parent == null) {
100 |           return;
101 |         }
102 | 
103 |         // Only allow JSDoc on VARs, function declarations, and assigns.
104 |         if (!parent.isVar() &&
105 |             !NodeUtil.isFunctionDeclaration(parent) &&
106 |             !(parent.isAssign() &&
107 |               n == parent.getFirstChild())) {
108 |           return;
109 |         }
110 | 
111 |         // There are four places the doc info could live.
112 |         // 1) A FUNCTION node.
113 |         // /** ... */ function f() { ... }
114 |         // 2) An ASSIGN parent.
115 |         // /** ... */ x = function () { ... }
116 |         // 3) A NAME parent.
117 |         // var x, /** ... */ y = function() { ... }
118 |         // 4) A VAR gramps.
119 |         // /** ... */ var x = function() { ... }
120 |         docInfo = n.getJSDocInfo();
121 |         if (docInfo == null &&
122 |             !(parent.isVar() &&
123 |                 !parent.hasOneChild())) {
124 |           docInfo = parent.getJSDocInfo();
125 |         }
126 | 
127 |         // Try to find the type of the NAME.
128 |         JSType varType = n.getJSType();
129 |         if (varType == null && parent.isFunction()) {
130 |           varType = parent.getJSType();
131 |         }
132 | 
133 |         // If we have no type to attach JSDocInfo to, then there's nothing
134 |         // we can do.
135 |         if (varType == null || docInfo == null) {
136 |           return;
137 |         }
138 | 
139 |         // Dereference the type. If the result is not an object, or already
140 |         // has docs attached, then do nothing.
141 |         ObjectType objType = dereferenceToObject(varType);
142 |         if (objType == null || objType.getJSDocInfo() != null) {
143 |           return;
144 |         }
145 | 
146 |         attachJSDocInfoToNominalTypeOrShape(objType, docInfo, n.getString());
147 |         break;
148 | 
149 |       case Token.GETPROP:
150 |         // Infer JSDocInfo on properties.
151 |         // There are two ways to write doc comments on a property.
152 |         //
153 |         // 1)
154 |         // /** @deprecated */
155 |         // obj.prop = ...
156 |         //
157 |         // 2)
158 |         // /** @deprecated */
159 |         // obj.prop;
160 |         if (parent.isExprResult() ||
161 |             (parent.isAssign() &&
162 |              parent.getFirstChild() == n)) {
163 |           docInfo = n.getJSDocInfo();
164 |           if (docInfo == null) {
165 |             docInfo = parent.getJSDocInfo();
166 |           }
167 |           if (docInfo != null) {
168 |             ObjectType lhsType =
169 |                 dereferenceToObject(n.getFirstChild().getJSType());
170 |             if (lhsType != null) {
171 |               // Put the JSDoc in the property slot, if there is one.
172 |               String propName = n.getLastChild().getString();
173 |               if (lhsType.hasOwnProperty(propName)) {
174 |                 lhsType.setPropertyJSDocInfo(propName, docInfo);
175 |               }
176 | 
177 |               // Put the JSDoc in any constructors or function shapes as well.
178 |               ObjectType propType =
179 |                   dereferenceToObject(lhsType.getPropertyType(propName));
180 |               if (propType != null) {
181 |                 attachJSDocInfoToNominalTypeOrShape(
182 |                     propType, docInfo, n.getQualifiedName());
183 |               }
184 |             }
185 |           }
186 |         }
187 |         break;
188 |     }
189 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.processFunctionNode(FunctionNode)

766 |     @Override
767 |     Node processFunctionNode(FunctionNode functionNode) {
768 |       Name name = functionNode.getFunctionName();
769 |       Boolean isUnnamedFunction = false;
770 |       if (name == null) {
771 |         int functionType = functionNode.getFunctionType();
772 |         if (functionType != FunctionNode.FUNCTION_EXPRESSION) {
773 |           errorReporter.error(
774 |             "unnamed function statement",
775 |             sourceName,
776 |             functionNode.getLineno(), "", 0);
777 | 
778 |           // Return the bare minimum to put the AST in a valid state.
779 |           return newNode(Token.EXPR_RESULT, Node.newNumber(0));
780 |         }
781 |         name = new Name();
782 |         name.setIdentifier("");
783 |         isUnnamedFunction = true;
784 |       }
785 |       Node node = newNode(Token.FUNCTION);
786 |       // if the function has an inline return annotation, attach it
787 |       Node newName = transformNodeWithInlineJsDoc(name);
788 |       if (isUnnamedFunction) {
789 |         // Old Rhino tagged the empty name node with the line number of the
790 |         // declaration.
791 |         newName.setLineno(functionNode.getLineno());
792 |         // TODO(bowdidge) Mark line number of paren correctly.
793 |         // Same problem as below - the left paren might not be on the
794 |         // same line as the function keyword.
795 |         int lpColumn = functionNode.getAbsolutePosition() +
796 |             functionNode.getLp();
797 |         newName.setCharno(position2charno(lpColumn));
798 |         maybeSetLengthFrom(newName, name);
799 |       }
800 | 
801 |       node.addChildToBack(newName);
802 |       Node lp = newNode(Token.PARAM_LIST);
803 |       // The left paren's complicated because it's not represented by an
804 |       // AstNode, so there's nothing that has the actual line number that it
805 |       // appeared on.  We know the paren has to appear on the same line as the
806 |       // function name (or else a semicolon will be inserted.)  If there's no
807 |       // function name, assume the paren was on the same line as the function.
808 |       // TODO(bowdidge): Mark line number of paren correctly.
809 |       Name fnName = functionNode.getFunctionName();
810 |       if (fnName != null) {
811 |         lp.setLineno(fnName.getLineno());
812 |       } else {
813 |         lp.setLineno(functionNode.getLineno());
814 |       }
815 |       int lparenCharno = functionNode.getLp() +
816 |           functionNode.getAbsolutePosition();
817 | 
818 |       lp.setCharno(position2charno(lparenCharno));
819 |       for (AstNode param : functionNode.getParams()) {
820 |         Node paramNode = transformNodeWithInlineJsDoc(param);
821 |         // When in ideMode Rhino can generate a param list with only a single
822 |         // ErrorNode. This is transformed into an EMPTY node. Drop this node in
823 |         // ideMode to keep the AST in a valid state.
824 |         if (paramNode.isName()) {
825 |           lp.addChildToBack(paramNode);
826 |         } else {
827 |           // We expect this in ideMode or when there is an error handling
828 |           // destructuring parameter assignments which aren't supported
829 |           // (an error has already been reported).
830 |           Preconditions.checkState(
831 |               config.isIdeMode
832 |               || paramNode.isObjectLit()
833 |               || paramNode.isArrayLit());
834 |         }
835 |       }
836 |       node.addChildToBack(lp);
837 | 
838 |       Node bodyNode = transform(functionNode.getBody());
839 |       if (!bodyNode.isBlock()) {
840 |         // When in ideMode Rhino tries to parse some constructs the compiler
841 |         // doesn't support, repair it here. see Rhino's
842 |         // Parser#parseFunctionBodyExpr.
843 |         Preconditions.checkState(config.isIdeMode);
844 |         bodyNode = IR.block();
845 |       }
846 |       parseDirectives(bodyNode);
847 |       node.addChildToBack(bodyNode);
848 |      return node;
849 |     }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.parseDirectives(Node)

Original range: 609-624
Documentation:
/**
     * Parse the directives, encode them in the AST, and remove their nodes.
     *
     * For information on ES5 directives, see section 14.1 of
     * ECMA-262, Edition 5.
     *
     * It would be nice if Rhino would eventually take care of this for
     * us, but right now their directive-processing is a one-off.
     */
Method code:
609 |     private void parseDirectives(Node node) {
610 |       // Remove all the directives, and encode them in the AST.
611 |       Set<String> directives = null;
612 |       while (isDirective(node.getFirstChild())) {
613 |         String directive = node.removeFirstChild().getFirstChild().getString();
614 |         if (directives == null) {
615 |           directives = Sets.newHashSet(directive);
616 |         } else {
617 |           directives.add(directive);
618 |         }
619 |       }
620 | 
621 |       if (directives != null) {
622 |         node.setDirectives(directives);
623 |       }
624 |     }

208 |   }

===== M0900 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.IRFactory.TransformDispatcher.processStringLiteral(StringLiteral)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/IRFactory.java
Original range: 1078-1105
Documentation:

Method code:
1078 |     @Override
1079 |     Node processStringLiteral(StringLiteral literalNode) {
1080 |       String value = literalNode.getValue();
1081 |       Node n = newStringNode(value);
1082 |       if (value.indexOf('\u000B') != -1) {
1083 |         // NOTE(nicksantos): In JavaScript, there are 3 ways to
1084 |         // represent a vertical tab: \v, \x0B, \u000B.
1085 |         // The \v notation was added later, and is not understood
1086 |         // on IE. So we need to preserve it as-is. This is really
1087 |         // obnoxious, because we do not have a good way to represent
1088 |         // how the original string was encoded without making the
1089 |         // representation of strings much more complicated.
1090 |         //
1091 |         // To handle this, we look at the original source test, and
1092 |         // mark the string as \v-encoded or not. If a string is
1093 |         // \v encoded, then all the vertical tabs in that string
1094 |         // will be encoded with a \v.
1095 |         int start = literalNode.getAbsolutePosition();
1096 |         int end = start + literalNode.getLength();
1097 |         if (start < sourceString.length() &&
1098 |             (sourceString.substring(
1099 |                  start, Math.min(sourceString.length(), end))
1100 |              .indexOf("\\v") != -1)) {
1101 |           n.putBooleanProp(Node.SLASH_V, true);
1102 |         }
1103 |       }
1104 |       return n;
1105 |     }

===== M0906 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.computeFollowNode(Node,Node,ControlFlowAnalysis)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 692-789
Documentation:
/**
   * Computes the follow() node of a given node and its parent. There is a side
   * effect when calling this function. If this function computed an edge that
   * exists a FINALLY, it'll attempt to connect the fromNode to the outer
   * FINALLY according to the finallyMap.
   *
   * @param fromNode The original source node since {@code node} is changed
   *        during recursion.
   * @param node The node that follow() should compute.
   */
Method code:
692 |   private static Node computeFollowNode(
693 |       Node fromNode, Node node, ControlFlowAnalysis cfa) {
694 |     /*
695 |      * This is the case where:
696 |      *
697 |      * 1. Parent is null implies that we are transferring control to the end of
698 |      * the script.
699 |      *
700 |      * 2. Parent is a function implies that we are transferring control back to
701 |      * the caller of the function.
702 |      *
703 |      * 3. If the node is a return statement, we should also transfer control
704 |      * back to the caller of the function.
705 |      *
706 |      * 4. If the node is root then we have reached the end of what we have been
707 |      * asked to traverse.
708 |      *
709 |      * In all cases we should transfer control to a "symbolic return" node.
710 |      * This will make life easier for DFAs.
711 |      */
712 |     Node parent = node.getParent();
713 |     if (parent == null || parent.isFunction() ||
714 |         (cfa != null && node == cfa.root)) {
715 |       return null;
716 |     }
717 | 
718 |     // If we are just before a IF/WHILE/DO/FOR:
719 |     switch (parent.getType()) {
720 |       // The follow() of any of the path from IF would be what follows IF.
721 |       case Token.IF:
722 |         return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
723 |       case Token.CASE:
724 |       case Token.DEFAULT_CASE:
725 |         // After the body of a CASE, the control goes to the body of the next
726 |         // case, without having to go to the case condition.
727 |         if (parent.getNext() != null) {
728 |           if (parent.getNext().isCase()) {
729 |             return parent.getNext().getFirstChild().getNext();
730 |           } else if (parent.getNext().isDefaultCase()) {
731 |             return parent.getNext().getFirstChild();
732 |           } else {
733 |             Preconditions.checkState(false, "Not reachable");
734 |           }
735 |         } else {
736 |           return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
737 |         }
738 |         break;
739 |       case Token.FOR:
740 |         if (NodeUtil.isForIn(parent)) {
741 |           return parent;
742 |         } else {
743 |           return parent.getFirstChild().getNext().getNext();
744 |         }
745 |       case Token.WHILE:
746 |       case Token.DO:
747 |         return parent;
748 |       case Token.TRY:
749 |         // If we are coming out of the TRY block...
750 |         if (parent.getFirstChild() == node) {
751 |           if (NodeUtil.hasFinally(parent)) { // and have FINALLY block.
752 |             return computeFallThrough(parent.getLastChild());
753 |           } else { // and have no FINALLY.
754 |             return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
755 |           }
756 |         // CATCH block.
757 |         } else if (NodeUtil.getCatchBlock(parent) == node){
758 |           if (NodeUtil.hasFinally(parent)) { // and have FINALLY block.
759 |             return computeFallThrough(node.getNext());
760 |           } else {
761 |             return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
762 |           }
763 |         // If we are coming out of the FINALLY block...
764 |         } else if (parent.getLastChild() == node){
765 |           if (cfa != null) {
766 |             for (Node finallyNode : cfa.finallyMap.get(parent)) {
767 |               cfa.createEdge(fromNode, Branch.ON_EX, finallyNode);
768 |             }
769 |           }
770 |           return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
771 |         }
772 |     }
773 | 
774 |     // Now that we are done with the special cases follow should be its
775 |     // immediate sibling, unless its sibling is a function
776 |     Node nextSibling = node.getNext();
777 | 
778 |     // Skip function declarations because control doesn't get pass into it.
779 |     while (nextSibling != null && nextSibling.isFunction()) {
780 |       nextSibling = nextSibling.getNext();
781 |     }
782 | 
783 |     if (nextSibling != null) {
784 |       return computeFallThrough(nextSibling);
785 |     } else {
786 |       // If there are no more siblings, control is transferred up the AST.
787 |       return computeFollowNode(fromNode, parent, cfa);
788 |     }
789 |   }

===== M0909 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.PrepareAst.PrepareAnnotations.annotateCalls(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/PrepareAst.java
Original range: 157-179
Documentation:
/**
     * There are two types of calls we are interested in calls without explicit
     * "this" values (what we are call "free" calls) and direct call to eval.
     */
Method code:
157 |     private void annotateCalls(Node n) {
158 |       Preconditions.checkState(n.isCall());
159 | 
160 |       // Keep track of of the "this" context of a call.  A call without an
161 |       // explicit "this" is a free call.
162 |       Node first = n.getFirstChild();
163 | 
164 |       // ignore cast nodes.
165 |       while (first.isCast()) {
166 |         first = first.getFirstChild();
167 |       }
168 | 
169 |       if (!NodeUtil.isGet(first)) {
170 |         n.putBooleanProp(Node.FREE_CALL, true);
171 |       }
172 | 
173 |       // Keep track of the context in which eval is called. It is important
174 |       // to distinguish between "(0, eval)()" and "eval()".
175 |       if (first.isName() &&
176 |           "eval".equals(first.getString())) {
177 |         first.putBooleanProp(Node.DIRECT_EVAL, true);
178 |       }
179 |     }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.AstControlFlowGraph.getOptionalNodeComparator(boolean)

Method code:
1021 |     @Override
1022 |     /**
1023 |      * Returns a node comparator based on the pre-order traversal of the AST.
1024 |      * @param isForward x 'before' y in the pre-order traversal implies
1025 |      * x 'less than' y (if true) and x 'greater than' y (if false).
1026 |      */
1027 |     public Comparator<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>> getOptionalNodeComparator(
1028 |         boolean isForward) {
1029 |       if (isForward) {
1030 |         return new Comparator<DiGraphNode<Node, Branch>>() {
1031 |           @Override

/**
   * <p>Checks enum aliases.
   *
   * <p>We verify that the enum element type of the enum used
   * for initialization is a subtype of the enum element type of
   * the enum the value is being copied in.</p>
   *
   * <p>Example:</p>
   * <pre>var myEnum = myOtherEnum;</pre>

   * <p>Enum aliases are irregular, so we need special code for this :(</p>

===== M0949 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.AbstractScopeBuilder.isQualifiedNameInferred(String,Node,JSDocInfo,Node,JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 1674-1728
Documentation:
/**
     * Determines whether a qualified name is inferred.
     * NOTE(nicksantos): Determining whether a property is declared or not
     * is really really obnoxious.
     *
     * The problem is that there are two (equally valid) coding styles:
     *
     * (function() {
     *   /* The authoritative definition of goog.bar. /
     *   goog.bar = function() {};
     * })();
     *
     * function f() {
     *   goog.bar();
     *   /* Reset goog.bar to a no-op. /
     *   goog.bar = function() {};
     * }
     *
     * In a dynamic language with first-class functions, it's very difficult
     * to know which one the user intended without looking at lots of
     * contextual information (the second example demonstrates a small case
     * of this, but there are some really pathological cases as well).
     *
     * The current algorithm checks if either the declaration has
     * JsDoc type information, or @const with a known type,
     * or a function literal with a name we haven't seen before.
     */
Method code:
1674 |     private boolean isQualifiedNameInferred(
1675 |         String qName, Node n, JSDocInfo info,
1676 |         Node rhsValue, JSType valueType) {
1677 |       if (valueType == null) {
1678 |         return true;
1679 |       }
1680 | 
1681 |       // Prototypes of constructors and interfaces are always declared.
1682 |       if (qName != null && qName.endsWith(".prototype")) {
1683 |         String className = qName.substring(0, qName.lastIndexOf(".prototype"));
1684 |         Var slot = scope.getSlot(className);
1685 |         JSType classType = slot == null ? null : slot.getType();
1686 |         if (classType != null
1687 |             && (classType.isConstructor() || classType.isInterface())) {
1688 |           return false;
1689 |         }
1690 |       }
1691 | 
1692 |       boolean inferred = true;
1693 |       if (info != null) {
1694 |         inferred = !(info.hasType()
1695 |             || info.hasEnumParameterType()
1696 |             || (isConstantSymbol(info, n) && valueType != null
1697 |                 && !valueType.isUnknownType())
1698 |             || FunctionTypeBuilder.isFunctionTypeDeclaration(info));
1699 |       }
1700 | 
1701 |       if (inferred && rhsValue != null && rhsValue.isFunction()) {
1702 |         if (info != null) {
1703 |           return false;
1704 |         } else if (!scope.isDeclared(qName, false) &&
1705 |             n.isUnscopedQualifiedName()) {
1706 | 
1707 |           // Check if this is in a conditional block.
1708 |           // Functions assigned in conditional blocks are inferred.
1709 |           for (Node current = n.getParent();
1710 |                !(current.isScript() || current.isFunction());
1711 |                current = current.getParent()) {
1712 |             if (NodeUtil.isControlStructure(current)) {
1713 |               return true;
1714 |             }
1715 |           }
1716 | 
1717 |           // Check if this is assigned in an inner scope.
1718 |           // Functions assigned in inner scopes are inferred.
1719 |           AstFunctionContents contents =
1720 |               getFunctionAnalysisResults(scope.getRootNode());
1721 |           if (contents == null ||
1722 |               !contents.getEscapedQualifiedNames().contains(qName)) {
1723 |             return false;
1724 |           }
1725 |         }
1726 |       }
1727 |       return inferred;
1728 |     }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.ControlFlowAnalysis.computeFallThrough(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/ControlFlowAnalysis.java
Original range: 796-810
Documentation:
/**
   * Computes the destination node of n when we want to fallthrough into the
   * subtree of n. We don't always create a CFG edge into n itself because of
   * DOs and FORs.
   */
Method code:
796 |   static Node computeFallThrough(Node n) {
797 |     switch (n.getType()) {
798 |       case Token.DO:
799 |         return computeFallThrough(n.getFirstChild());
800 |       case Token.FOR:
801 |         if (NodeUtil.isForIn(n)) {
802 |           return n.getFirstChild().getNext();
803 |         }
804 |         return computeFallThrough(n.getFirstChild());
805 |       case Token.LABEL:
806 |         return computeFallThrough(n.getLastChild());
807 |       default:
808 |         return n;
809 |     }
810 |   }

===== M0955 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.JSTypeRegistry.createRecordTypeFromNodes(Node,String,StaticScope<JSType>)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/JSTypeRegistry.java
Original range: 1776-1827
Documentation:
/**
   * Creates a RecordType from the nodes representing said record type.
   * @param n The node with type info.
   * @param sourceName The source file name.
   * @param scope A scope for doing type name lookups.
   */
Method code:
1776 |   private JSType createRecordTypeFromNodes(Node n, String sourceName,
1777 |       StaticScope<JSType> scope) {
1778 | 
1779 |     RecordTypeBuilder builder = new RecordTypeBuilder(this);
1780 | 
1781 |     // For each of the fields in the record type.
1782 |     for (Node fieldTypeNode = n.getFirstChild();
1783 |          fieldTypeNode != null;
1784 |          fieldTypeNode = fieldTypeNode.getNext()) {
1785 | 
1786 |       // Get the property's name.
1787 |       Node fieldNameNode = fieldTypeNode;
1788 |       boolean hasType = false;
1789 | 
1790 |       if (fieldTypeNode.getType() == Token.COLON) {
1791 |         fieldNameNode = fieldTypeNode.getFirstChild();
1792 |         hasType = true;
1793 |       }
1794 | 
1795 |       String fieldName = fieldNameNode.getString();
1796 | 
1797 |       // TODO(user): Move this into the lexer/parser.
1798 |       // Remove the string literal characters around a field name,
1799 |       // if any.
1800 |       if (fieldName.startsWith("'") || fieldName.startsWith("\"")) {
1801 |         fieldName = fieldName.substring(1, fieldName.length() - 1);
1802 |       }
1803 | 
1804 |       // Get the property's type.
1805 |       JSType fieldType = null;
1806 | 
1807 |       if (hasType) {
1808 |         // We have a declared type.
1809 |         fieldType = createFromTypeNodesInternal(
1810 |             fieldTypeNode.getLastChild(), sourceName, scope);
1811 |       } else {
1812 |         // Otherwise, the type is UNKNOWN.
1813 |         fieldType = getNativeType(JSTypeNative.UNKNOWN_TYPE);
1814 |       }
1815 | 
1816 |       // Add the property to the record.
1817 |       if (builder.addProperty(fieldName, fieldType, fieldNameNode) == null) {
1818 |         // Duplicate field name, warning and skip
1819 |         reporter.warning(
1820 |             "Duplicate record field " + fieldName,
1821 |             sourceName,
1822 |             n.getLineno(), fieldNameNode.getCharno());
1823 |       }
1824 |     }
1825 | 
1826 |     return builder.build();
1827 |   }

   * Expect the type to be anything but the null or void type. If the
   * expectation is not met, issue a warning at the provided node's
   * source code position. Note that a union type that includes the
   * void type and at least one other type meets the expectation.
   * @return Whether the expectation was met.
   */
Method code:
257 |   boolean expectNotNullOrUndefined(
258 |       NodeTraversal t, Node n, JSType type, String msg, JSType expectedType) {
259 |     if (!type.isNoType() && !type.isUnknownType() &&
260 |         type.isSubtype(nullOrUndefined) &&
261 |         !containsForwardDeclaredUnresolvedName(type)) {
262 | 
263 |       // There's one edge case right now that we don't handle well, and
264 |       // that we don't want to warn about.
265 |       // if (this.x == null) {
266 |       //   this.initializeX();
267 |       //   this.x.foo();
268 |       // }
269 |       // In this case, we incorrectly type x because of how we
270 |       // infer properties locally. See issue 109.
271 |       // http://code.google.com/p/closure-compiler/issues/detail?id=109
272 |       //
273 |       // We do not do this inference globally.
274 |       if (n.isGetProp() &&
275 |           !t.inGlobalScope() && type.isNullType()) {
276 |         return true;
277 |       }
278 | 
279 |       mismatch(t, n, msg, type, expectedType);
280 |       return false;
281 |     }
282 |     return true;
283 |   }

===== M0968 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeInference.branchedFlowThrough(Node,FlowScope)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeInference.java
Original range: 192-305
Documentation:

Method code:
192 |   @Override
193 |   @SuppressWarnings({"fallthrough", "incomplete-switch"})
194 |   List<FlowScope> branchedFlowThrough(Node source, FlowScope input) {
195 |     // NOTE(nicksantos): Right now, we just treat ON_EX edges like UNCOND
196 |     // edges. If we wanted to be perfect, we'd actually JOIN all the out
197 |     // lattices of this flow with the in lattice, and then make that the out
198 |     // lattice for the ON_EX edge. But it's probably too expensive to be
199 |     // worthwhile.
200 |     FlowScope output = flowThrough(source, input);
201 |     Node condition = null;
202 |     FlowScope conditionFlowScope = null;
203 |     BooleanOutcomePair conditionOutcomes = null;
204 | 
205 |     List<DiGraphEdge<Node, Branch>> branchEdges = getCfg().getOutEdges(source);
206 |     List<FlowScope> result = Lists.newArrayListWithCapacity(branchEdges.size());
207 |     for (DiGraphEdge<Node, Branch> branchEdge : branchEdges) {
208 |       Branch branch = branchEdge.getValue();
209 |       FlowScope newScope = output;
210 | 
211 |       switch (branch) {
212 |         case ON_TRUE:
213 |           if (NodeUtil.isForIn(source)) {
214 |             // item is assigned a property name, so its type should be string.
215 |             Node item = source.getFirstChild();
216 |             Node obj = item.getNext();
217 | 
218 |             FlowScope informed = traverse(obj, output.createChildFlowScope());
219 | 
220 |             if (item.isVar()) {
221 |               item = item.getFirstChild();
222 |             }
223 |             if (item.isName()) {
224 |               JSType iterKeyType = getNativeType(STRING_TYPE);
225 |               ObjectType objType = getJSType(obj).dereference();
226 |               JSType objIndexType = objType == null ?
227 |                   null : objType.getTemplateTypeMap().getTemplateType(
228 |                       registry.getObjectIndexKey());
229 |               if (objIndexType != null && !objIndexType.isUnknownType()) {
230 |                 JSType narrowedKeyType =
231 |                     iterKeyType.getGreatestSubtype(objIndexType);
232 |                 if (!narrowedKeyType.isEmptyType()) {
233 |                   iterKeyType = narrowedKeyType;
234 |                 }
235 |               }
236 |               redeclareSimpleVar(informed, item, iterKeyType);
237 |             }
238 |             newScope = informed;
239 |             break;
240 |           }
241 | 
242 |           // FALL THROUGH
243 | 
244 |         case ON_FALSE:
245 |           if (condition == null) {
246 |             condition = NodeUtil.getConditionExpression(source);
247 |             if (condition == null && source.isCase()) {
248 |               condition = source;
249 | 
250 |               // conditionFlowScope is cached from previous iterations
251 |               // of the loop.
252 |               if (conditionFlowScope == null) {
253 |                 conditionFlowScope = traverse(
254 |                     condition.getFirstChild(), output.createChildFlowScope());
255 |               }
256 |             }
257 |           }
258 | 
259 |           if (condition != null) {
260 |             if (condition.isAnd() ||
261 |                 condition.isOr()) {
262 |               // When handling the short-circuiting binary operators,
263 |               // the outcome scope on true can be different than the outcome
264 |               // scope on false.
265 |               //
266 |               // TODO(nicksantos): The "right" way to do this is to
267 |               // carry the known outcome all the way through the
268 |               // recursive traversal, so that we can construct a
269 |               // different flow scope based on the outcome. However,
270 |               // this would require a bunch of code and a bunch of
271 |               // extra computation for an edge case. This seems to be
272 |               // a "good enough" approximation.
273 | 
274 |               // conditionOutcomes is cached from previous iterations
275 |               // of the loop.
276 |               if (conditionOutcomes == null) {
277 |                 conditionOutcomes = condition.isAnd() ?
278 |                     traverseAnd(condition, output.createChildFlowScope()) :
279 |                     traverseOr(condition, output.createChildFlowScope());
280 |               }
281 |               newScope =
282 |                   reverseInterpreter.getPreciserScopeKnowingConditionOutcome(
283 |                       condition,
284 |                       conditionOutcomes.getOutcomeFlowScope(
285 |                           condition.getType(), branch == Branch.ON_TRUE),
286 |                       branch == Branch.ON_TRUE);
287 |             } else {
288 |               // conditionFlowScope is cached from previous iterations
289 |               // of the loop.
290 |               if (conditionFlowScope == null) {
291 |                 conditionFlowScope =
292 |                     traverse(condition, output.createChildFlowScope());
293 |               }
294 |               newScope =
295 |                   reverseInterpreter.getPreciserScopeKnowingConditionOutcome(
296 |                       condition, conditionFlowScope, branch == Branch.ON_TRUE);
297 |             }
298 |           }
299 |           break;
300 |       }
301 | 
302 |       result.add(newScope.optimize());
303 |     }
304 |     return result;
305 |   }

297 |   public boolean isDict() {

/**
   * Concatenates two ImmutableList instances. If either input is empty, the
   * other is returned; otherwise, a new ImmutableList instance is created that
   * contains the contents of both arguments.
   */
Method code:
307 |   private <T> ImmutableList<T> concatImmutableLists(
308 |     ImmutableList<T> first, ImmutableList<T> second) {

===== M0981 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeValidator.registerMismatch(JSType,JSType,JSError)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeValidator.java
Original range: 671-695
Documentation:

Method code:
671 |   private void registerMismatch(JSType found, JSType required, JSError error) {
672 |     // Don't register a mismatch for differences in null or undefined or if the
673 |     // code didn't downcast.
674 |     found = found.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
675 |     required = required.restrictByNotNullOrUndefined();
676 |     if (found.isSubtype(required) || required.isSubtype(found)) {
677 |       return;
678 |     }
679 | 
680 |     mismatches.add(new TypeMismatch(found, required, error));
681 |     if (found.isFunctionType() &&
682 |         required.isFunctionType()) {
683 |       FunctionType fnTypeA = found.toMaybeFunctionType();
684 |       FunctionType fnTypeB = required.toMaybeFunctionType();
685 |       Iterator<Node> paramItA = fnTypeA.getParameters().iterator();
686 |       Iterator<Node> paramItB = fnTypeB.getParameters().iterator();
687 |       while (paramItA.hasNext() && paramItB.hasNext()) {
688 |         registerIfMismatch(paramItA.next().getJSType(),
689 |             paramItB.next().getJSType(), error);
690 |       }
691 | 
692 |       registerIfMismatch(
693 |           fnTypeA.getReturnType(), fnTypeB.getReturnType(), error);
694 |     }
695 |   }

===== M1005 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypedScopeCreator.FirstOrderFunctionAnalyzer.visit(NodeTraversal,Node,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypedScopeCreator.java
Original range: 2120-2171
Documentation:

Method code:
2120 |     @Override public void visit(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
2121 |       if (t.inGlobalScope()) {
2122 |         return;
2123 |       }
2124 | 
2125 |       if (n.isReturn() && n.getFirstChild() != null) {
2126 |         data.get(t.getScopeRoot()).recordNonEmptyReturn();
2127 |       }
2128 | 
2129 |       if (t.getScopeDepth() <= 1) {
2130 |         // The first-order function analyzer looks at two types of variables:
2131 |         //
2132 |         // 1) Local variables that are assigned in inner scopes ("escaped vars")
2133 |         //
2134 |         // 2) Local variables that are assigned more than once.
2135 |         //
2136 |         // We treat all global variables as escaped by default, so there's
2137 |         // no reason to do this extra computation for them.
2138 |         return;
2139 |       }
2140 | 
2141 |       if (n.isName() && NodeUtil.isLValue(n) &&
2142 |           // Be careful of bleeding functions, which create variables
2143 |           // in the inner scope, not the scope where the name appears.
2144 |           !NodeUtil.isBleedingFunctionName(n)) {
2145 |         String name = n.getString();
2146 |         Scope scope = t.getScope();
2147 |         Var var = scope.getVar(name);
2148 |         if (var != null) {
2149 |           Scope ownerScope = var.getScope();
2150 |           if (ownerScope.isLocal()) {
2151 |             data.get(ownerScope.getRootNode()).recordAssignedName(name);
2152 |           }
2153 | 
2154 |           if (scope != ownerScope && ownerScope.isLocal()) {
2155 |             data.get(ownerScope.getRootNode()).recordEscapedVarName(name);
2156 |           }
2157 |         }
2158 |       } else if (n.isGetProp() && n.isUnscopedQualifiedName() &&
2159 |           NodeUtil.isLValue(n)) {
2160 |         String name = NodeUtil.getRootOfQualifiedName(n).getString();
2161 |         Scope scope = t.getScope();
2162 |         Var var = scope.getVar(name);
2163 |         if (var != null) {
2164 |           Scope ownerScope = var.getScope();
2165 |           if (scope != ownerScope && ownerScope.isLocal()) {
2166 |             data.get(ownerScope.getRootNode())
2167 |                 .recordEscapedQualifiedName(n.getQualifiedName());
2168 |           }
2169 |         }
2170 |       }
2171 |     }

===== M1011 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeCheck.visitFunction(NodeTraversal,Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeCheck.java
Original range: 1693-1762
Documentation:
/**
   * Visits a {@link Token#FUNCTION} node.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal object that supplies context, such as the
   * scope chain to use in name lookups as well as error reporting.
   * @param n The node being visited.
   */
Method code:
1693 |   private void visitFunction(NodeTraversal t, Node n) {
1694 |     FunctionType functionType = JSType.toMaybeFunctionType(n.getJSType());
1695 |     String functionPrivateName = n.getFirstChild().getString();
1696 |     if (functionType.isConstructor()) {
1697 |       FunctionType baseConstructor = functionType.getSuperClassConstructor();
1698 |       if (baseConstructor != getNativeType(OBJECT_FUNCTION_TYPE) &&
1699 |           baseConstructor != null &&
1700 |           baseConstructor.isInterface()) {
1701 |         compiler.report(
1702 |             t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_EXTENDED_TYPE,
1703 |                         "constructor", functionPrivateName));
1704 |       } else {
1705 |         if (baseConstructor != getNativeType(OBJECT_FUNCTION_TYPE)) {
1706 |           ObjectType proto = functionType.getPrototype();
1707 |           if (functionType.makesStructs() && !proto.isStruct()) {
1708 |             compiler.report(t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_SHAPE_TYPE,
1709 |                                         "struct", functionPrivateName));
1710 |           } else if (functionType.makesDicts() && !proto.isDict()) {
1711 |             compiler.report(t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_SHAPE_TYPE,
1712 |                                         "dict", functionPrivateName));
1713 |           }
1714 |         }
1715 |         // All interfaces are properly implemented by a class
1716 |         for (JSType baseInterface : functionType.getImplementedInterfaces()) {
1717 |           boolean badImplementedType = false;
1718 |           ObjectType baseInterfaceObj = ObjectType.cast(baseInterface);
1719 |           if (baseInterfaceObj != null) {
1720 |             FunctionType interfaceConstructor =
1721 |               baseInterfaceObj.getConstructor();
1722 |             if (interfaceConstructor != null &&
1723 |                 !interfaceConstructor.isInterface()) {
1724 |               badImplementedType = true;
1725 |             }
1726 |           } else {
1727 |             badImplementedType = true;
1728 |           }
1729 |           if (badImplementedType) {
1730 |             report(t, n, BAD_IMPLEMENTED_TYPE, functionPrivateName);
1731 |           }
1732 |         }
1733 |         // check properties
1734 |         validator.expectAllInterfaceProperties(t, n, functionType);
1735 |       }
1736 |     } else if (functionType.isInterface()) {
1737 |       // Interface must extend only interfaces
1738 |       for (ObjectType extInterface : functionType.getExtendedInterfaces()) {
1739 |         if (extInterface.getConstructor() != null
1740 |             && !extInterface.getConstructor().isInterface()) {
1741 |           compiler.report(
1742 |               t.makeError(n, CONFLICTING_EXTENDED_TYPE,
1743 |                           "interface", functionPrivateName));
1744 |         }
1745 |       }
1746 | 
1747 |       // Check whether the extended interfaces have any conflicts
1748 |       if (functionType.getExtendedInterfacesCount() > 1) {
1749 |         // Only check when extending more than one interfaces
1750 |         HashMap<String, ObjectType> properties
1751 |             = new HashMap<String, ObjectType>();
1752 |         HashMap<String, ObjectType> currentProperties
1753 |             = new HashMap<String, ObjectType>();
1754 |         for (ObjectType interfaceType : functionType.getExtendedInterfaces()) {
1755 |           currentProperties.clear();
1756 |           checkInterfaceConflictProperties(t, n, functionPrivateName,
1757 |               properties, currentProperties, interfaceType);
1758 |           properties.putAll(currentProperties);
1759 |         }
1760 |       }
1761 |     }
1762 |   }

===== M1014 =====

483 |     @Override
484 |     public final boolean shouldTraverse(NodeTraversal t, Node n, Node parent) {
485 |       inputId = t.getInputId();
486 |       if (n.isFunction() ||
487 |           n.isScript()) {
488 |         Preconditions.checkNotNull(inputId);
489 |         sourceName = NodeUtil.getSourceName(n);
490 |       }
491 | 
492 |       // We do want to traverse the name of a named function, but we don't
493 |       // want to traverse the arguments or body.
494 |       boolean descend = parent == null || !parent.isFunction() ||
495 |           n == parent.getFirstChild() || parent == scope.getRootNode();
496 |

498 |         // Handle hoisted functions on pre-order traversal, so that they
499 |         // get hit before other things in the scope.

501 |           for (Node child = n.getFirstChild();
502 |                child != null;

505 |               defineFunctionLiteral(child);
506 |             }
507 |           }
508 |         }
509 |       }
510 | 
511 |       return descend;
512 |     }

===== M1020 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.TypeInferencePass.inferAllScopes(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/TypeInferencePass.java
Original range: 77-112
Documentation:
/** Entry point for type inference when running over part of the tree. */
Method code:
77 |   void inferAllScopes(Node node) {
78 |     // Type analysis happens in two major phases.
79 |     // 1) Finding all the symbols.
80 |     // 2) Propagating all the inferred types.
81 |     //
82 |     // The order of this analysis is non-obvious. In a complete inference
83 |     // system, we may need to backtrack arbitrarily far. But the compile-time
84 |     // costs would be unacceptable.
85 |     //
86 |     // We do one pass where we do typed scope creation for all scopes
87 |     // in pre-order.
88 |     //
89 |     // Then we do a second pass where we do all type inference
90 |     // (type propagation) in pre-order.
91 |     //
92 |     // We use a memoized scope creator so that we never create a scope
93 |     // more than once.
94 |     //
95 |     // This will allow us to handle cases like:
96 |     // var ns = {};
97 |     // (function() { /** JSDoc */ ns.method = function() {}; })();
98 |     // ns.method();
99 |     // In this code, we need to build the symbol table for the inner scope in
100 |     // order to propagate the type of ns.method in the outer scope.
101 |     (new NodeTraversal(
102 |         compiler, new FirstScopeBuildingCallback(), scopeCreator))
103 |         .traverseWithScope(node, topScope);
104 | 
105 |     for (Scope s : scopeCreator.getAllMemoizedScopes()) {
106 |       s.resolveTypes();
107 |     }
108 | 
109 |     (new NodeTraversal(
110 |         compiler, new SecondScopeBuildingCallback(), scopeCreator))
111 |         .traverseWithScope(node, topScope);
112 |   }

===== M1025 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.ArrowType.isSubtype(JSType)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/ArrowType.java
Original range: 79-171
Documentation:

Method code:
79 |   @Override
80 |   public boolean isSubtype(JSType other) {
81 |     if (!(other instanceof ArrowType)) {
82 |       return false;
83 |     }
84 | 
85 |     ArrowType that = (ArrowType) other;
86 | 
87 |     // This is described in Draft 2 of the ES4 spec,
88 |     // Section 3.4.7: Subtyping Function Types.
89 | 
90 |     // this.returnType <: that.returnType (covariant)
91 |     if (!this.returnType.isSubtype(that.returnType)) {
92 |       return false;
93 |     }
94 | 
95 |     // that.paramType[i] <: this.paramType[i] (contravariant)
96 |     //
97 |     // If this.paramType[i] is required,
98 |     // then that.paramType[i] is required.
99 |     //
100 |     // In theory, the "required-ness" should work in the other direction as
101 |     // well. In other words, if we have
102 |     //
103 |     // function f(number, number) {}
104 |     // function g(number) {}
105 |     //
106 |     // Then f *should* not be a subtype of g, and g *should* not be
107 |     // a subtype of f. But in practice, we do not implement it this way.
108 |     // We want to support the use case where you can pass g where f is
109 |     // expected, and pretend that g ignores the second argument.
110 |     // That way, you can have a single "no-op" function, and you don't have
111 |     // to create a new no-op function for every possible type signature.
112 |     //
113 |     // So, in this case, g < f, but f !< g
114 |     Node thisParam = parameters.getFirstChild();
115 |     Node thatParam = that.parameters.getFirstChild();
116 |     while (thisParam != null && thatParam != null) {
117 |       JSType thisParamType = thisParam.getJSType();
118 |       JSType thatParamType = thatParam.getJSType();
119 |       if (thisParamType != null) {
120 |         if (thatParamType == null ||
121 |             !thatParamType.isSubtype(thisParamType)) {
122 |           return false;
123 |         }
124 |       }
125 | 
126 |       boolean thisIsVarArgs = thisParam.isVarArgs();
127 |       boolean thatIsVarArgs = thatParam.isVarArgs();
128 |       boolean thisIsOptional = thisIsVarArgs || thisParam.isOptionalArg();
129 |       boolean thatIsOptional = thatIsVarArgs || thatParam.isOptionalArg();
130 | 
131 |       // "that" can't be a supertype, because it's missing a required argument.
132 |       if (!thisIsOptional && thatIsOptional) {
133 |         // NOTE(nicksantos): In our type system, we use {function(...?)} and
134 |         // {function(...NoType)} to to indicate that arity should not be
135 |         // checked. Strictly speaking, this is not a correct formulation,
136 |         // because now a sub-function can required arguments that are var_args
137 |         // in the super-function. So we special-case this.
138 |         boolean isTopFunction =
139 |             thatIsVarArgs &&
140 |             (thatParamType == null ||
141 |              thatParamType.isUnknownType() ||
142 |              thatParamType.isNoType());
143 |         if (!isTopFunction) {
144 |           return false;
145 |         }
146 |       }
147 | 
148 |       // don't advance if we have variable arguments
149 |       if (!thisIsVarArgs) {
150 |         thisParam = thisParam.getNext();
151 |       }
152 |       if (!thatIsVarArgs) {
153 |         thatParam = thatParam.getNext();
154 |       }
155 | 
156 |       // both var_args indicates the end
157 |       if (thisIsVarArgs && thatIsVarArgs) {
158 |         thisParam = null;
159 |         thatParam = null;
160 |       }
161 |     }
162 | 
163 |     // "that" can't be a supertype, because it's missing a required argument.
164 |     if (thisParam != null
165 |         && !thisParam.isOptionalArg() && !thisParam.isVarArgs()
166 |         && thatParam == null) {
167 |       return false;
168 |     }
169 | 
170 |     return true;
171 |   }

===== M1032 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.rhino.jstype.ObjectType.isUnknownType()
Source: src/com/google/javascript/rhino/jstype/ObjectType.java
Original range: 552-572
Documentation:
/**
   * We treat this as the unknown type if any of its implicit prototype
   * properties is unknown.
   */
Method code:
552 |   @Override
553 |   public boolean isUnknownType() {
554 |     // If the object is unknown now, check the supertype again,
555 |     // because it might have been resolved since the last check.
556 |     if (unknown) {
557 |       ObjectType implicitProto = getImplicitPrototype();
558 |       if (implicitProto == null ||
559 |           implicitProto.isNativeObjectType()) {
560 |         unknown = false;
561 |         for (ObjectType interfaceType : getCtorExtendedInterfaces()) {
562 |           if (interfaceType.isUnknownType()) {
563 |             unknown = true;
564 |             break;
565 |           }
566 |         }
567 |       } else {
568 |         unknown = implicitProto.isUnknownType();
569 |       }
570 |     }
571 |     return unknown;
572 |   }

===== M1038 =====
Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.SyntacticScopeCreator.scanRoot(Node)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/SyntacticScopeCreator.java
Original range: 72-107
Documentation:

Method code:
72 |   private void scanRoot(Node n) {
73 |     if (n.isFunction()) {
74 |       if (inputId == null) {
75 |         inputId = NodeUtil.getInputId(n);
76 |         // TODO(johnlenz): inputId maybe null if the FUNCTION node is detached
77 |         // from the AST.
78 |         // Is it meaningful to build a scope for detached FUNCTION node?
79 |       }
80 | 
81 |       final Node fnNameNode = n.getFirstChild();
82 |       final Node args = fnNameNode.getNext();
83 |       final Node body = args.getNext();
84 | 
85 |       // Bleed the function name into the scope, if it hasn't
86 |       // been declared in the outer scope.
87 |       String fnName = fnNameNode.getString();
88 |       if (!fnName.isEmpty() && NodeUtil.isFunctionExpression(n)) {
89 |         declareVar(fnNameNode);
90 |       }
91 | 
92 |       // Args: Declare function variables
93 |       Preconditions.checkState(args.isParamList());
94 |       for (Node a = args.getFirstChild(); a != null;
95 |            a = a.getNext()) {
96 |         Preconditions.checkState(a.isName());
97 |         declareVar(a);
98 |       }
99 | 
100 |       // Body
101 |       scanVars(body);
102 |     } else {
103 |       // It's the global block
104 |       Preconditions.checkState(scope.getParent() == null);
105 |       scanVars(n);
106 |     }
107 |   }

Signature: com.google.javascript.jscomp.parsing.JsDocInfoParser.parseTypeName(JsDocToken)
Source: src/com/google/javascript/jscomp/parsing/JsDocInfoParser.java
Original range: 1925-1960
Documentation:
/**
   * TypeName := NameExpression | NameExpression TypeApplication
   * TypeApplication := '.<' TypeExpressionList '>'
   */
Method code:
1925 |   private Node parseTypeName(JsDocToken token) {
1926 |     if (token != JsDocToken.STRING) {
1927 |       return reportGenericTypeSyntaxWarning();
1928 |     }
1929 | 
1930 |     String typeName = stream.getString();
1931 |     int lineno = stream.getLineno();
1932 |     int charno = stream.getCharno();
1933 |     while (match(JsDocToken.EOL) &&
1934 |         typeName.charAt(typeName.length() - 1) == '.') {
1935 |       skipEOLs();
1936 |       if (match(JsDocToken.STRING)) {
1937 |         next();
1938 |         typeName += stream.getString();
1939 |       }
1940 |     }
1941 | 
1942 |     Node typeNameNode = newStringNode(typeName, lineno, charno);
1943 | 
1944 |     if (match(JsDocToken.LT)) {
1945 |       next();
1946 |       skipEOLs();
1947 |       Node memberType = parseTypeExpressionList(next());
1948 |       if (memberType != null) {
1949 |         typeNameNode.addChildToFront(memberType);
1950 | 
1951 |         skipEOLs();
1952 |         if (!match(JsDocToken.GT)) {
1953 |           return reportTypeSyntaxWarning("msg.jsdoc.missing.gt");
1954 |         }
1955 | 
1956 |         next();
1957 |       }
1958 |     }
1959 |     return typeNameNode;
1960 |   }

/**
   * Expect that the first type can be assigned to a symbol of the second
   * type.
   *
   * @param t The node traversal.
   * @param n The node to issue warnings on.
   * @param rightType The type on the RHS of the assign.
   * @param leftType The type of the symbol on the LHS of the assign.
   * @param owner The owner of the property being assigned to.
   * @param propName The name of the property being assigned to.

Method code:
367 |   boolean expectCanAssignToPropertyOf(NodeTraversal t, Node n, JSType rightType,
368 |       JSType leftType, Node owner, String propName) {
369 |     // The NoType check is a hack to make typedefs work OK.
370 |     if (!leftType.isNoType() && !rightType.isSubtype(leftType)) {

379 |           return true;
380 |         }
381 |       }
382 |

384 |           "assignment to property " + propName + " of " +
385 |           getReadableJSTypeName(owner, true),

387 |       return false;
388 |     }
